Cool-fact-is-answer: the named witness whose pamphlet led to the Red Cross and the Geneva Conventions. A Western institution born of conscience (imago-Dei lineage). Story-led, courage/compassion shown not preached.

**26. Fort Sumter and the outbreak of the Civil War** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T2-T3 · wt=high ⭐  
April 1861 first shots, secession crisis · *src: HISTORY_FRAMEWORK Pillar 2 Civil War; APUSH*

The secession crisis framed accurately — the seceding states named slavery as the cause (see the Cornerstone/causes topic). State plainly, no false 'states' rights in the abstract' dodge.

**27. Causes of the Civil War and the Cornerstone Speech** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T4-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
Slavery as the cause, Stephens 1861, secession declarations · *src: HISTORY_FRAMEWORK Pillar 2; APUSH*  
> FLAGSHIP truth-telling topic per framework Pillar 2 + §3 forbidden-list (no 'states' rights' euphemism). The war was fought over slavery, in the secessionists' OWN words: Alexander Stephens's 1861 Cornerstone Speech ('its corner-stone rests upon the great truth that the negro is not equal to the white man; that slavery... is his natural and normal condition') and the secession declarations of Mississippi, South Carolina, Texas. Cool-fact-is-answer: the actual stated cause, quoted. Honest history, stated plainly, no sanitizing (framework forbidden #4). HEAVY, vision-mandated.

Hampton Roads March 1862, end of wooden warships · *src: HISTORY_TEMPLATES Pillar 2 Civil War*

> Pillar 2 + 3. Cool-fact-is-answer: the deadliest single day in American history (~23,000 casualties, Sept 17 1862), and the lost-orders twist (Lee's Special Order 191 found wrapped around cigars). Bloody Lane, Burnside's Bridge. State the carnage plainly — honest, not sanitized. The victory that let Lincoln issue Emancipation. American story deep.

**30. The Emancipation Proclamation** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=high ⭐

Cool-fact-is-answer: the named act and its precise scope (it freed the enslaved in the rebelling states, making the war explicitly about ending slavery and opening Black enlistment). Honest about its limits (it didn't free the border states — the 13th Amendment did that) without cynicism. Natural-rights fulfillment. Vision-mandated.

Cool-fact-is-answer: the high-water-mark of the Confederacy — Pickett's Charge across open ground into Union guns July 3 1863, Chamberlain's bayonet charge down Little Round Top. The turning point. Story-led, scene-set, stakes + consequence. American story deep, drama-rich.

**36. Frederick Douglass and the abolitionists** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=high ⭐

> KEYSTONE — Douglass is named explicitly in moral_vision §1, §3.6 and §8 as a bank exemplar (the Rochester July 5 1852 'What to the Slave is the Fourth of July?' speech). Cool-fact-is-answer: his exact argument — NOT that the Declaration was wrong, but that America was betraying its own true founding principles (the sophisticated natural-rights move, quoted in moral_vision §8 as the bar). The escaped slave who taught himself to read and became America's conscience. Virtue at cost. HIGHEST weight.

**37. Harriet Tubman and the Underground Railroad** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T2-T4 · wt=high ⭐

> Pillar 2, named in framework §2. Virtue-by-example (courage at cost, moral_vision §2). Cool-fact-is-answer: 'I never lost a passenger' — the conductor who returned to slave territory roughly 13 times to free ~70 people, carried a pistol, later scouted for the Union army at the Combahee Raid. Show the courage, don't preach it. American story.

**38. Reconstruction and the Reconstruction Amendments** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T4-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
13th/14th/15th Amendments, KKK, Jim Crow's rise · *src: HISTORY_FRAMEWORK Pillar 2; APUSH*  
> Pillar 2 — 'the unfinished struggle over what freedom would mean'. Cool-fact-is-answer: the three amendments and what each did (13th abolished slavery, 14th equal protection/citizenship, 15th the vote regardless of race), then the honest tragedy: the KKK's terror and Jim Crow's rise as the promise was rolled back. State the failure plainly per moral_vision §3.6 (American failures stated as fact, not theatricalized) — and frame it as betrayal of the founding principles, the Douglass/Lincoln natural-rights line, NOT a 1619-style indictment of the founding itself (framework forbidden #3).

Honor the Chinese (Central Pacific) and Irish (Union Pacific) labor that built it as the achievement-by-real-people it was (moral_vision ordinary-ingenuity), stating the dangerous conditions plainly without anti-Western framing.

**40. Westward expansion and the Plains Indian Wars** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=medium ⭐  
Little Bighorn 1876, Custer, Sitting Bull, Crazy Horse · *src: HISTORY_TEMPLATES Pillar 3; APUSH*

Cool-fact-is-answer: Sitting Bull's vision and Crazy Horse's tactics annihilating Custer's command at Little Bighorn 1876, then the tragic close at Wounded Knee 1890. Present the Plains nations on their own terms (skilled, formidable), the conquest's human cost as fact — NOT 'savages' framing, NOT theatricalized self-loathing. Honest comparison, both the military drama and the displacement.

**41. The Gilded Age industrialists** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=medium  
Carnegie steel, Rockefeller oil, Vanderbilt rails · *src: HISTORY_TEMPLATES Pillar 2 late-19th; APUSH*

Cool-fact-is-answer: the named achievement (Carnegie rising from bobbin boy, the Gospel of Wealth and his libraries; Rockefeller's refining efficiency that cut kerosene prices). Steel-man both the creative and the predatory; avoid the lazy 'robber baron' caricature AND avoid hagiography.

**42. The Scramble for Africa and the Berlin Conference** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T4-T5 · wt=medium ⭐  
1884-85 partition, European powers carve a continent · *src: AP World Unit 6 state expansion; cultural literacy*  
> Honest record stated plainly (moral_vision §4 'state what happened, don't editorialize'). Cool-fact-is-answer: Europe carving up a continent with rulers on a map at the Berlin Conference 1884-85, no African present; the named atrocity floor — Leopold II's Congo Free State (rubber terror, severed hands, millions dead) as a documented horror. State the colonial violence as fact per §3.4 'part of the same honest record'. No anti-Western inherent-condemnation framing (§9) — name the specific perpetrators and the specific Western critics who exposed it (E.D. Morel, Roger Casement).

**43. The British Raj in India** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=medium ⭐  
1857 rebellion, Crown rule 1858, jewel in the crown · *src: HISTORY_TEMPLATES Pillar 5 India; AP World Unit 6*  
> Honest dual record (moral_vision §1.7, §3.4) — the 1857 Rebellion (Sepoy Mutiny, the greased-cartridge spark), Crown rule 1858, the 'jewel in the crown'. State both: the railways/rule-of-law/English-language institutions AND the famines, the extraction. Pairs with framework §5 — Bentinck banning suttee 1829 under Carey's pressure, Sleeman suppressing Thuggee: honest pre-modern brutality ended by named reformers (decomposed into its own injected cell). Present India's civilization on its own terms, not as foil.

**44. The Opium Wars and China's century of humiliation** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T4-T5 · wt=medium ⭐  
1839-42 + 1856-60, treaty ports, Hong Kong ceded · *src: HISTORY_TEMPLATES Pillar 5 China; AP World Unit 6*  
> Honest record stated plainly — Britain forcing a drug trade on China by gunboat (moral_vision §4). Cool-fact-is-answer: Commissioner Lin Zexu dumping 1,000+ tons of opium into the sea at Canton 1839, the British response, Hong Kong ceded, the treaty ports. State the British wrong as fact, no euphemism — this is honest about Western failure per §3.4. Present China's grievance on its own terms (the 'century of humiliation'). Sets up the Taiping and the later Chinese story.

**45. Taiping Rebellion** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T4-T5 · wt=medium ⭐  
1850-64, 20M+ dead, Christian-messianic uprising · *src: HISTORY_TEMPLATES Pillar 5 China*

Cool-fact-is-answer: Hong Xiuquan, the failed examination candidate who believed he was Jesus's younger brother and launched a civil war that killed more than the entire American Civil War many times over. Present on its own terms, the staggering toll stated as fact. A scale-of-suffering most kids never hear of.

**46. Commodore Perry's black ships open Japan** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T3-T4 · wt=medium  
1853 fleet ends sakoku isolation · *src: HISTORY_TEMPLATES Pillar 5 Japan; AP World*  
> Pillar 5 — American warships ending 250 years of Japanese seclusion. Cool-fact-is-answer: Perry's black ships steaming into Edo Bay 1853, the gunboat diplomacy that cracked sakoku open. Present on its own terms; the hinge that triggers the Meiji Restoration. Story-led, named moment.

**47. The Meiji Restoration and Japan's modernization** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T4-T5 · wt=medium ⭐  
1868 restoration, rapid industrialization, end of samurai · *src: HISTORY_TEMPLATES Pillar 5 Japan; AP World Unit 6*  
> Pillar 5 — a nation that industrialized in a single generation, on its own terms (moral_vision §4, non-Western civilizations as real achievements not foils). Cool-fact-is-answer: the 1868 restoration of the emperor, the deliberate wholesale adoption of Western industry/law/military, and the end of the samurai (the Satsuma Rebellion, the last stand). A genuinely astonishing self-transformation. Substantive, admiring, on Japan's own terms.

**48. Social Darwinism and the civilizing-mission rationale** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T4-T5 · wt=medium ⭐  
'Survival of fittest' applied to nations, white man's burden · *src: AP World Unit 6.1 rationales for imperialism*  
> Present the IDEOLOGY for recognition, attributed to its proponents, NOT adopted (moral_vision §6 advocacy-framing rule fires hard here). Frame as: the rationalizations Europeans USED to justify ruling others — Spencer's 'survival of the fittest' misapplied to nations, Kipling's 'white man's burden'. The bank teaches the kid to RECOGNIZE this as the pseudo-scientific self-justification it was (§3.7 — biological variance never extrapolated to group moral worth; this is the historical example of exactly that error). Honest history of a bad idea, named and dated, never endorsed and never used to indict the West as inherently evil (§9) — it indicts a specific false ideology.

**49. Adwa: Ethiopia defeats Italy** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T4-T5 · wt=medium ⭐  
1896 victory, only African state to repel conquest · *src: HISTORY_TEMPLATES Pillar 5 Africa*  
> Pillar 5 — an African army crushing a European invasion, on its own terms (moral_vision §4). Cool-fact-is-answer: Menelik II and Empress Taytu's forces routing the Italians at Adwa 1896, keeping Ethiopia the one African state never colonized in the Scramble. A genuine military triumph told straight, the named victory as the answer. Present Ethiopia's achievement admiringly.

**50. The Suez Canal** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T3-T4 · wt=low  
1869 opening, link of Mediterranean and Red Sea · *src: AP World Unit 6; cultural literacy*  
> Pillar 1/6 engineering wonder + geopolitics. Cool-fact-is-answer: de Lesseps's canal opening 1869 joining the Mediterranean and Red Sea, cutting the voyage to India by thousands of miles, and Britain's later purchase of the shares that made it imperial lifeline. Mechanism + consequence. Present on its own terms.

**51. Rorke's Drift and the Anglo-Zulu War** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T3-T4 · wt=low  
1879 Isandlwana defeat then 11 Victoria Crosses · *src: HISTORY_TEMPLATES Pillar 3 19th-c battles*  
> Pillar 3 iconic moment, BOTH sides honored. Cool-fact-is-answer: the Zulu impi annihilating a British column at Isandlwana 1879 (a spear-armed kingdom shocking the Empire), then ~150 men holding the mission station at Rorke's Drift, earning 11 Victoria Crosses. Present the Zulu as the formidable, disciplined army they were (not foil) AND the defenders' stand. Story-led drama, named victories on both sides.

**52. Darwin and On the Origin of Species** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=medium ⭐  
1859 evolution by natural selection, Galapagos finches · *src: HISTORY_TEMPLATES Pillar 1; AP World/cultural literacy*  
> Pillar 1/6 — the book that transformed how we see life. Cool-fact-is-answer: natural selection, the Galapagos finches, the Beagle voyage, and the drama (Darwin sitting on the theory 20 years, then Wallace's letter forcing the joint 1858 announcement). Discovery-recognition (how-we-know). Present the science straight, mechanism-wonder. NOTE moral_vision treats science and faith as non-opponents (§3.4) — present Darwin as discovery, not as a weapon against religion; keep contemporary creation/evolution policy debates OUT (dated-topical rule).

**53. Pasteur, germ theory, and vaccines** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=medium  
Germ theory, anthrax 1881, rabies vaccine 1885 · *src: HISTORY_TEMPLATES Pillar 6 science*  
> Pillar 6 discovery + mechanism wonder (the immune mechanism is REAL proven biology — honored per the science-stance memory). Cool-fact-is-answer: Pasteur proving germs cause disease (the swan-neck flask defeating spontaneous generation), the anthrax field trial 1881, the rabies vaccine saving young Joseph Meister 1885. Discovery-recognition, virtue (the public risk of the field trial). Keep it to the 19th-c scientific achievement — do NOT import contemporary vaccine-policy contestation (that's science-bank, present-day, out of this historical strand).

**54. The Eiffel Tower and the 1889 World's Fair** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T2-T3 · wt=low  
Built for exposition, slated for demolition · *src: HISTORY_TEMPLATES Pillar 1 art*  
> Pillar 1 art/architecture + surprise-reversal. Cool-fact-is-answer: the iron icon built for the 1889 Exposition, denounced by Paris artists, slated for demolition after 20 years — saved because it made a perfect radio antenna. Wonder of engineering + the reversal that it was almost torn down. Light, celebratory; Western achievement.

**55. Seneca Falls and the women's rights movement** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=medium  
1848 convention, Declaration of Sentiments, suffrage · *src: HISTORY_FRAMEWORK Pillar 2; APUSH*  
> Pillar 2 — the organized fight for women's rights born from the natural-rights tradition. Cool-fact-is-answer: the 1848 Seneca Falls Convention and the Declaration of Sentiments deliberately echoing the Declaration of Independence ('all men and women are created equal'), Stanton and Mott, Douglass the lone prominent man backing the suffrage resolution. Frame as the founding principles being extended/claimed — the same Douglass natural-rights move, NOT a grievance-against-the-founding frame. Present on its own terms.

**56. The Industrial Revolution as the Great Enrichment — the hockey-stick escape from poverty** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T4-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
~1800 inflection: living standards, life expectancy, and real wages begin their unprecedented sustained rise after millennia of flat subsistence; market capital accumulation as the engine · *src: vision-injected*  
> The consensus list itemizes the inventions but MISSES the headline the vision demands: industrialization was the most important good-news event in human history — the moment ordinary people began escaping the grinding poverty universal for all prior history. Cool-fact-is-answer: the named inflection (the 'hockey stick' of GDP/life-expectancy bending sharply up around 1800), the surprise-reversal that the 'dark satanic mills' era was when child mortality and starvation began their long collapse. Market-driven capital accumulation as the engine (moral_vision §3.3). Wonder-first, honestly stated — the human cost (child labor) is covered in its own cells; this cell carries the progress the standard curriculum buries under the misery narrative. Attribute interpretive claims; let the data carry it, no triumphalism.

**57. Bastiat and the seen-versus-unseen — the broken-window fallacy (1850)** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T4-T5 · wt=medium ⭐  
Frederic Bastiat, 'That Which Is Seen and That Which Is Not Seen' 1850, the broken-window parable, the satirical candlemakers' petition · *src: vision-injected*  
> Present the reasoning move for recognition, attributed to Bastiat ('Bastiat argued...'). Cool-fact-is-answer: the broken-window parable (breaking a window does NOT enrich the town — it merely hides the bread or book the shopkeeper would otherwise have bought) and the satirical Candlemakers' Petition demanding protection from the unfair competition of the sun. The 19th-century French economist who is the bank's named exemplar of seen/unseen reasoning. Teach-before-test honored (introduce Bastiat as the figure). Wonder-driven, not polemical.

**58. The internal African slave trade and the Royal Navy West Africa Squadron** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T4-T5 · wt=medium ⭐  
Africans enslaving Africans for coastal kingdoms (Asante, Dahomey) and the Arab-Swahili trade (Tippu Tip's Zanzibar network into the 1890s); the Royal Navy squadron 1808-1860s freeing ~150,000 at sea; Livingstone's documentation · *src: vision-injected*  
> Honest record stated plainly per framework §5, NO 'savages' framing, NO sensationalism. State the fact the standard curriculum omits: the slave trade had a large internal-African and Arab-Swahili supply side that long predated and outlasted the Atlantic trade (Tippu Tip operating into the 1890s), and it was the Royal Navy West Africa Squadron — at real cost in sailors' lives to malaria — that hunted slavers across the Atlantic 1808-1860s, freeing roughly 150,000 people. Pair with Livingstone exposing the East African trade. The Wilberforce cell legislates abolition; THIS cell is the enforcement and the honest supply-side record (§3.4 imago-Dei crusade in action).

**59. Suttee, Thuggee, and foot-binding ended by named reformers** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T4-T5 · wt=medium ⭐  
Hindu widow-burning banned by Lord William Bentinck 1829 under William Carey's pressure; the Thuggee strangulation cult suppressed by William Sleeman in the 1830s-40s; Chinese foot-binding ended by missionary-reformer collaboration · *src: vision-injected*  
> Honest record, stated as fact, NO condescension and NO 'exotic East' framing (moral_vision §4) — paired with the named reformers who ended each practice. Cool-fact-is-answer: Bentinck's 1829 ban on suttee, Sleeman's detective campaign that broke the Thuggee cult (possibly 2M murders over centuries), the missionary-Chinese-reformer alliance against foot-binding. These are 19th-century reforms (Bentinck 1829, Sleeman 1830s-40s) squarely in this strand. Honest comparison, not condescension (§4): some traditions developed the reform impulse, and the bank says so by naming who acted — without inherent-condemnation of any culture (§9).

**60. Cyrus Field and the Atlantic telegraph cable** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T3-T4 · wt=medium ⭐  
The 1858 and successful 1866 transatlantic cables; instant Europe-America communication; repeated failures before triumph; the Great Eastern laying it · *src: vision-injected*  
> Pillar 1/6 wonder + virtue-by-example (perseverance through repeated failure). Cool-fact-is-answer: Cyrus Field's transatlantic cable — the 1858 line that worked for weeks then died, and the 1866 success that finally let a message cross the ocean in minutes instead of the ten days a ship took. Lead with the wonder of the Atlantic annihilated and the drama of the failed attempts. A staking-everything-on-a-calculation story the vision prizes (cf. the Wright brothers).

**61. Faraday, Maxwell, and the conquest of electromagnetism** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T4-T5 · wt=medium ⭐  
Faraday's induction (1831) and field concept; Maxwell's equations (1865) unifying electricity, magnetism, and light; both devout Christians · *src: vision-injected*  
> Pillar 6 mechanism-wonder + Western-tradition-as-achievement. Cool-fact-is-answer: Faraday — the self-taught bookbinder's apprentice who discovered electromagnetic induction (move a magnet near a wire, get a current: the principle behind every generator) — and Maxwell predicting from pure equations that light itself IS an electromagnetic wave, unifying three phenomena. Both were serious Christians (moral_vision §3.4 names them); present the science straight, the faith as biographical fact, never as opponents. The theoretical foundation that made the entire electrical age (Edison, Tesla) possible. Beauty-of-mechanism, why it works.

**62. Mendel and the birth of genetics** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T3-T4 · wt=medium ⭐  
Gregor Mendel's pea-plant experiments (1856-1863) establishing the laws of inheritance; work ignored until rediscovered ~1900 · *src: vision-injected*  
> Pillar 6 discovery + surprise-reversal + Western-tradition-as-achievement. Cool-fact-is-answer: the Augustinian friar Gregor Mendel counting tens of thousands of pea plants in a monastery garden to discover the hidden math of heredity (dominant and recessive traits) — work so far ahead of its time it was ignored for 35 years until three botanists independently rediscovered it in 1900. A monk founding genetics (moral_vision §3.4 names Mendel; Christianity and science as non-opponents). Discovery + the reversal of the overlooked genius.


## World War I & the interwar years (48)

**1. Assassination of Archduke Franz Ferdinand** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T1-T4 · wt=high  
Sarajevo 1914 spark of WWI · *src: Core Knowledge, AP World/Euro, encyclopedias*  
> Story-led per voice rules: the wrong turn down Franz Josef Street, Princip stepping off the curb, the chain of contingency. The cool fact is the answer (the sandwich-shop wrong turn, the failed grenade earlier that morning) per HISTORY_FRAMEWORK 'cool fact is the answer.' No 'this shows how fragile peace was' weasel closer (moral_vision anti-pattern; SHARED §15). Present the human accident, not a thesis about determinism.

**2. The July Crisis and alliance system** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T2-T5 · wt=medium  
Alliances drag Europe into war · *src: AP Euro, AP World, Core Knowledge*  
> Mechanism over slogan: how mobilization timetables and the blank-check ultimatum turned a Balkan murder into a continental war. Steelman the decisions of real statesmen under real pressure (framework core principle) rather than 'everyone blundered.' Avoid the abstract 'entangling alliances' lecture; anchor to a named moment (Austrian ultimatum to Serbia, German blank cheque). No verdict-imposing stem on war-guilt — present the cascade.

**3. Trench warfare and the Western Front** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T1-T5 · wt=high

**43. The Holodomor — Stalin's terror-famine in Ukraine** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T4-T5 · wt=high ⭐

Count it honestly: collectivization tripled grain quotas, borders sealed so starving villagers couldn't flee, peasants caught gleaning dropped grain were shot, 3.5-7M Ukrainians dead. CRITICAL named figures (the cool-fact answers): Walter Duranty won the 1932 Pulitzer reporting it as 'exaggerated propaganda' while privately conceding to the British embassy 'perhaps 10 million' had died — Pulitzer never revoked; Gareth Jones broke the truth honestly and was murdered in Mongolia 1935. No false equivalence, no soft-pedaling; distractors include the establishment-minimized scale. This is the bank's signature truth-telling cell.

**44. Lenin's Red Terror and the founding of the Gulag** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T4-T5 · wt=high ⭐

> REQUIRED to correct the romantic 'Russian Revolution' frame. The terror did NOT begin with Stalin: Lenin's Cheka (Dzerzhinsky) launched the Red Terror in 1918, the Tambov rebellion was crushed with poison gas, hostage-taking and class-extermination decrees, and the first concentration camps (Solovki) date to 1918-1923. Solzhenitsyn's central thesis (the cool-fact answer): the Gulag was a CORE FEATURE of the Soviet state from its founding, designed not accidental — the camp system Stalin merely scaled. State the Bolshevik violence against peasants, priests, and rival socialists plainly (moral_vision §4 show-the-evil). Steelman nothing about the terror itself.

> REQUIRED so the Nazi-Soviet Pact is not told as Germany's crime alone. When the USSR took its half of Poland in Sept 1939, the NKVD murdered ~22,000 Polish officers, police, and intelligentsia in the Katyn Forest (and other sites) spring 1940 — then blamed the Nazis for decades; the USSR admitted it only in 1990. The named atrocity (Katyn) is the cool-fact answer. Also: the forced annexation of Estonia, Latvia, Lithuania 1940 and the Winter War against Finland 1939-40. State Soviet aggression as established fact with no false equivalence (moral_vision §3.1) — the bank refuses to launder the USSR's 1939-41 record because it later joined the Allies.

1937 mass atrocity, ~200,000-300,000 killed · *src: vision-injected*  
> REQUIRED, decomposed from 'Rise of militarist Japan' because a mass atrocity of this scale must stand as its own named cell, not a clause. State plainly: over six weeks from Dec 1937, Japanese forces killed an estimated 200,000-300,000 Chinese civilians and prisoners in the captured Nationalist capital, with mass executions and systematic atrocity. The cool-fact answer hook (moral_vision §2 ordinary-courage-by-example): John Rabe, a German businessman and Nazi Party member, used his swastika armband's protective status to shelter ~250,000 Chinese in the Nanking Safety Zone — a startling case of one man's courage. Show the evil; honor the rescuer; no euphemism (framework forbids sanitizing).

**47. Interwar Western breakthroughs — the expanding universe and penicillin** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=medium ⭐  
Lemaître's Big Bang, Hubble, Fleming's 1928 accident, the dawn of modern physics · *src: vision-injected*  
> REQUIRED counterweight: the bank's HEAVIEST pillar (Western tradition as achievement) is nearly absent from the consensus interwar list, which is almost all war and dictatorship. The 1920s-30s were also a golden age of Western discovery. Cool-fact answers: Georges Lemaître — a Belgian Catholic PRIEST — proposed in 1927 what became the Big Bang (moral_vision §3.4: modern science largely the work of believers), confirmed by Hubble's 1929 redshift law; Alexander Fleming's accidental 1928 penicillin mould on a Petri dish (ordinary ingenuity, §2); the quantum revolution (Heisenberg's uncertainty 1927, the Solvay 1927 photograph of Einstein-Bohr-Curie). Wonder-first, the strange thing led. This restores the inheritance-worth-transmitting balance the strand otherwise loses.

**48. Coolidge prosperity and the limited-government 1920s** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T4-T5 · wt=low ⭐  
The decade's real growth under low taxes and light regulation · *src: vision-injected*  
> REQUIRED so the American interwar story is not ONLY the New Deal expansion of government. Present as fact, not advocacy (moral_vision §6, no-verdict-on-contested): the 1920s saw real, broad-based prosperity, electrification, and the rise of the automobile and consumer economy under the Harding-Coolidge tax cuts (Mellon's rate reductions) and limited federal footprint. The named specific (Coolidge's 'the chief business of the American people is business'; the Mellon tax cuts) is the cool-fact answer. Balances the consensus's heavy New-Deal/positive-government tilt by making the limited-government, sound-money, dynamic-growth account of the era available — steelmanned, factual, not a sermon.


## World War II (57)

**1. Appeasement and the Munich Agreement** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=high  
Chamberlain, 'peace for our time', Sudetenland 1938 · *src: AP Euro Unit 8; Core Knowledge*  
> Steel-man the appeasers, then tell the truth (moral_vision §6): Chamberlain was not a fool but a man haunted by the Somme who genuinely believed he was sparing a generation. Present that sincerely, then state the result — the carve-up of a democracy emboldened the aggressor. Churchill's 'you were given the choice between war and dishonour; you chose dishonour and you will have war' belongs here as the named counter-voice.

**2. Invasion of Poland and Blitzkrieg** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T2-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
Sept 1 1939 war begins; lightning-war tactics · *src: AP World Unit 7; Britannica*  
> Story-led, the beauty-of-mechanism angle on the tactic (moral_vision §2): combined arms — Stukas, Panzers, radio — collapsing a defense that fought the last war. CRITICAL vision overlay: Poland was invaded from BOTH sides — Germany Sept 1, the Soviet Union Sept 17 under the secret pact. An Anglo-centric telling omits the eastern half; the bank does not (anti-totalitarianism in every form).

**3. Nazi-Soviet (Molotov-Ribbentrop) Pact** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T4-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
Aug 1939 Hitler-Stalin secret carve-up of Poland · *src: AP World/Euro Unit 7/8*  
> VISION-LOAD-BEARING (moral_vision §1.2, §3.1; HISTORY_FRAMEWORK line 146 'honest about Soviet role'). The two totalitarian powers were collaborators before they were enemies — the secret protocol divided Poland, the Baltics, and Finland. State that Stalin's USSR was an active partner in starting the war in the East, supplied Germany with grain and oil until June 1941, and only 'switched sides' when invaded. No false equivalence-erasure that paints Stalin as a pure victim-ally from the start.

**4. Fall of France 1940** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=medium  
Six-week defeat, Maginot Line bypassed, occupation · *src: AP Euro Unit 8; Britannica*  
> Surprise-reversal energy (moral_vision §2): the strongest army in the West on paper collapsed in six weeks because doctrine, not courage, failed — the Ardennes 'impassable' to tanks. Present on its own terms; the drama of a great power's sudden fall is the hook. The human cost of occupation stated plainly without editorializing.

**5. Dunkirk Evacuation** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T2-T5 · wt=high  
1940 'little ships' rescue 338,000 from beaches · *src: Core Knowledge; AP Euro; Britannica*  
> Ordinary ingenuity + courage celebrated (moral_vision §2): fishing boats, pleasure yachts and lifeboats crewed by civilians crossing the Channel under fire to lift an army off the sand. This is virtue-through-example — show the deed, never preach it. 'Deliverance' was Churchill's own word; pair it with his warning that 'wars are not won by evacuations.'

**6. Battle of Britain** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T2-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
1940 RAF vs Luftwaffe; radar; 'the Few' · *src: AP World/Euro Unit 7/8; Britannica*  
> Western tradition defending itself + beauty of mechanism (moral_vision §2, §3.3): the Dowding System — radar chain, plotting tables, ground control — as a real technological-organizational achievement, not just brave pilots. 'Never in the field of human conflict was so much owed by so many to so few' is the named quote. A free people holding the line alone against tyranny: celebrate it honestly.

**7. The Blitz** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T2-T4 · wt=medium  
London bombed nightly 1940-41; civilian resilience · *src: Core Knowledge; AP Euro; Britannica*  
> Courage under fire shown, not sentimentalized (moral_vision §5): the Underground shelters, St Paul's standing amid the smoke (the famous photograph), Coventry's cathedral gutted Nov 1940. Tell the home-front endurance straight. Avoid both jingoism and the cynical 'myth of the Blitz' debunking frame; state what people actually did.

**8. Winston Churchill's Wartime Leadership** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T2-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
'Blood, toil, tears and sweat'; defiant speeches · *src: Core Knowledge; AP Euro; cultural-literacy canon*  
> Canonical Western moral-courage figure (moral_vision §2 'courage in pursuit of truth', §3.6). The voice that refused terms when surrender looked rational — 'we shall fight on the beaches', 'never surrender'. Celebrate the defiance and the oratory honestly. The bank may note he was a complex, flawed man (Gallipoli, his imperial views) without theatricalizing it — flawed humans who did extraordinary things, per the Founders rule applied generally.

**9. Battle of the Atlantic and U-Boats** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=medium  
Convoys vs wolfpacks; supply lifeline to Britain · *src: AP World Unit 7; Britannica*  
> Mechanism + ordinary endurance (moral_vision §2): the convoy system, sonar/ASDIC, escort carriers, and the merchant seamen who sailed knowing the odds. Churchill said the U-boat peril was the only thing that ever truly frightened him — use that. The codebreaking link (Ultra reading U-boat traffic) connects to Bletchley as a genuine science-wins-wars thread.

**10. North Africa and El Alamein** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=medium  
Rommel the 'Desert Fox' vs Montgomery, 1942 turn · *src: AP Euro Unit 8; Britannica*  
> Story-led generalship (HISTORY_FRAMEWORK pillar 3). Present Rommel on his own terms as a formidable and relatively chivalrous opponent (the desert war's reputation as a 'war without hate') — honest assessment, not demonization of every German soldier. Churchill: 'Before Alamein we never had a victory. After Alamein we never had a defeat.' The turning-point framing is the hook.

**11. Operation Barbarossa** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
1941 German invasion of USSR; largest in history · *src: AP World/Euro Unit 7/8; Britannica*  
> Anti-totalitarianism in every form (moral_vision §1.2): two totalitarian empires now at war with each other. State the scale honestly AND its character — this was a war of annihilation and racial ideology, with the Einsatzgruppen following the front lines (the 'Holocaust by bullets'). The kid should see Hitler's fatal two-front gamble AND that the Eastern Front was the war's deadliest and most ideologically savage theater.

**12. Siege of Leningrad** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T4-T5 · wt=medium  
872-day blockade; ~1M dead; ice 'Road of Life' · *src: AP Euro Unit 8; Britannica*  
> Human endurance stated plainly (moral_vision §4 'honest, not preachy'). A starving city for 872 days, the frozen Lake Ladoga supply route, Shostakovich's Seventh Symphony performed by skeletal musicians and broadcast at the Germans. Celebrate the endurance of the people; the bank can honor Russian suffering and courage while never laundering Stalin's regime — the two are separable and the vision keeps them separate.

**13. Battle of Stalingrad** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=high  
1942-43 turning point; German 6th Army destroyed · *src: AP World/Euro Unit 7/8; Britannica*  
> The eastern hinge of the war, told straight (HISTORY_FRAMEWORK pillar 3). Honor the staggering courage and sacrifice of the soldiers and the city. Vision overlay: do NOT romanticize the Soviet command — Order No. 227 'Not One Step Back', blocking detachments shooting their own retreating men, are part of the honest record. Soviet soldiers fought heroically AND were driven by a murderous regime; both true.

**14. Battle of Kursk** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T4-T5 · wt=medium  
1943 largest tank battle; Soviets seize initiative · *src: AP Euro Unit 8; Britannica*  
> Mechanism + decisive-moment hook (HISTORY_FRAMEWORK pillar 3): the greatest clash of armor in history, Prokhorovka, the moment Germany permanently lost the initiative in the East. Present on its own terms — the technical drama of Tigers vs T-34s and the strategic consequence. Ultra intelligence's role in forewarning the Soviets is a fair codebreaking thread.

**15. Pearl Harbor** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T1-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
Dec 7 1941 surprise attack; 'day of infamy' · *src: Core Knowledge Gr8; AP US/World; Britannica*  
> The American story (moral_vision §2, §3.6; HISTORY_FRAMEWORK pillar 2). The attack that brought the United States into the war it would help win. FDR's 'a date which will live in infamy' is the named anchor. Tell it as the pivot that mobilized the arsenal of democracy. State plainly without the contested 'FDR knew' conspiracy framing (out of scope per the no-flame-bait rule).

**16. Bataan Death March** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T3-T5 · wt=medium  
1942 brutal forced march of POWs in Philippines · *src: Core Knowledge Gr8; AP US*  
> Atrocity stated as fact (moral_vision §4 'state what happened'). The brutal treatment of surrendered American and Filipino troops is part of the honest record of the Pacific war. No sensationalism, no condescension toward Japan-as-culture — state the specific conduct plainly, the way the bank states all atrocities.

**17. Doolittle Raid** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T3-T4 · wt=low  
1942 first US bombers strike Tokyo off carriers · *src: AP US; Britannica*  
> Daring + ingenuity celebrated (moral_vision §2): launching land bombers off a carrier deck, a one-way mission flown for morale when America needed hope. Virtue-through-deed. The strategic ripple (it helped push Japan toward the Midway gamble) is a satisfying mechanism payoff.

**18. Battle of the Coral Sea** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T4-T5 · wt=low  
1942 first carrier battle; ships never saw each other · *src: AP World Unit 7; Britannica*  
> Beauty of mechanism / surprise-reversal (moral_vision §2): the first naval battle in history where the opposing fleets never sighted each other — warfare changed forever, decided by aircraft over the horizon. The wonder hook is the strangeness of the new kind of battle.

**19. Battle of Midway** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
June 1942 four Japanese carriers sunk; Pacific turn · *src: AP World Unit 7; Britannica*  
> The decisive Pacific turn, story-led (HISTORY_FRAMEWORK pillar 3). The codebreaking that set the ambush (Station Hypo cracking the 'AF' target), the five fatal minutes when American dive-bombers caught the carriers with planes on deck. Honest celebration of an American victory won by intelligence, courage, and a measure of luck — name all three.

**20. Guadalcanal Campaign** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T4-T5 · wt=low  
1942-43 first US ground offensive in the Pacific · *src: AP US; Britannica*  
> American story / endurance (HISTORY_FRAMEWORK pillar 2): the grueling first land offensive, the naval battles off Savo Island, the Marines holding Henderson Field. Tell the slog straight as the start of the long road back. Present on its own terms.

**21. Island-Hopping Strategy** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=medium  
Leapfrogging Japanese strongholds toward the homeland · *src: Core Knowledge Gr8; AP US/World*  
> The winning American plan, mechanism-first (HISTORY_FRAMEWORK pillar 2): bypassing fortified islands to let them 'wither on the vine' rather than assaulting each one. A clever strategic idea explained clearly — the kind of ordinary-genius-applied-at-scale the bank loves.

**22. Battle of Iwo Jima** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T2-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
1945 brutal island fight; flag-raising photo · *src: Core Knowledge Gr8; AP US; Britannica*  
> American courage, iconic image (moral_vision §2; HISTORY_FRAMEWORK pillar 2). Rosenthal's flag-raising on Suribachi as a genuine cultural-canon photograph. 'Uncommon valor was a common virtue' — Nimitz's line — is virtue-through-example done for you. Honor the Marines' sacrifice; state the ferocity plainly.

**23. Battle of Okinawa** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T4-T5 · wt=medium  
1945 last great battle; kamikazes; mass casualties · *src: AP US; Britannica*  
> Honest record of the Pacific endgame (moral_vision §4). The mass casualties — military and Okinawan civilian — and the kamikaze onslaught made this the bloody preview that directly shaped the atomic-bomb calculation. State the human cost plainly; this topic is the factual bridge to the Hiroshima decision, so the casualty reality must be concrete.

**24. Kamikaze Pilots** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T3-T4 · wt=low  
Japanese suicide-plane attacks on Allied ships · *src: AP World Unit 7; Britannica*  
> Present on its own terms, no condescension (moral_vision §4). Explain the tactic and the desperation behind it — a regime spending its young men as guided weapons — without exoticizing or mocking. The recognition skill is understanding how a militarist ideology demanded this; honest, not sensational.

**25. D-Day / Normandy Invasion** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T1-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
June 6 1944 largest seaborne invasion; Western front · *src: Core Knowledge Gr8; AP US/World; Britannica*  
> The liberation of Western Europe — top-billing American/Allied story (moral_vision §2, §3.6; HISTORY_FRAMEWORK pillar 2). The scale, the deception (Operation Fortitude), the Mulberry harbors, the bluffs of Omaha Beach, Eisenhower's pre-drafted note taking sole blame had it failed (a stunning act of integrity — use it). Celebrate the deed; honor the dead; state the cost.

**26. Liberation of Paris** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T3-T4 · wt=low  
Aug 1944 Paris freed; De Gaulle's return · *src: AP Euro Unit 8; Britannica*  
> Liberation moment told straight (moral_vision §2). The emotional reclaiming of an occupied capital, the role of the French Resistance and the Free French. Vision overlay: honor the Resistance as virtue-through-deed, but the bank does not airbrush the messier aftermath (the réglement de comptes) — state plainly if it comes up, without dwelling.

**27. Battle of the Bulge** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=medium  
1944-45 last German offensive; 'Nuts!' at Bastogne · *src: Core Knowledge Gr8; AP US; Britannica*  
> American story / courage at cost (HISTORY_FRAMEWORK pillar 2). The surprise winter offensive, the surrounded 101st at Bastogne, McAuliffe's one-word reply 'Nuts!' to the German surrender demand — a named, kid-memorable moment. Vision overlay: the Malmedy massacre of American POWs is part of the honest record of this battle. Celebrate the stand; state the atrocity.

**28. Fall of Berlin and Hitler's Death** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
1945 Soviet assault; Hitler's suicide in the bunker · *src: AP Euro Unit 8; Britannica*  
> The end of the Third Reich, stated plainly (moral_vision §4). Hitler's suicide in the bunker, the regime's collapse. Vision overlay (HISTORY_FRAMEWORK line 146 'honest about Soviet role'): the fall of Berlin also began the mass rapes and brutality of the Soviet occupation and the descent of the Iron Curtain over the East — the seed of the next moral conflict. Don't end the European story on a clean triumphalist note that hides what came next for half of Europe.

**29. Kristallnacht** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T3-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
1938 'Night of Broken Glass' pogrom against Jews · *src: Core Knowledge; AP Euro; Holocaust curricula*  
> Holocaust documented unflinchingly (moral_vision §4; HISTORY_FRAMEWORK line 146). The state-organized pogrom — synagogues burned, shops smashed, 30,000 Jewish men sent to camps — as the turn from legal persecution to open, organized violence. State it plainly. The recognition skill: how a regime escalated by stages, and how the world's silence registered.

**30. Nazi Ghettos (Warsaw Ghetto Uprising)** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
Jews walled into ghettos; 1943 Warsaw revolt · *src: AP Euro Unit 8; Holocaust curricula*  
> Courage at cost / virtue-through-deed (moral_vision §2, §4). The 1943 Warsaw Ghetto Uprising — starving, near-unarmed fighters holding off the SS for weeks, knowing they would lose — is one of history's purest acts of defiance. Honor it as such. State the confinement and the deportations plainly; the resistance is the wonder, the atrocity is the honest record. (Distinct from the 1944 Warsaw Rising of the Home Army — keep them separate.)

**31. The Final Solution and Wannsee Conference** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T4-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
1942 bureaucratic plan to exterminate Europe's Jews · *src: AP Euro/World; Holocaust curricula*  
> Anti-totalitarianism foundational (moral_vision §1.2, §4). The chilling fact the bank does NOT flinch from: genocide organized as cold bureaucracy — a 90-minute meeting over lunch where officials coordinated the murder of a continent's Jews. The 'banality of evil' recognition (Arendt) — that ordinary clerks administered mass murder — is the load-bearing lesson. State it plainly; no euphemism.

**32. Auschwitz and the Death Camps** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
Industrialized murder; gas chambers; ~6M Jews killed · *src: Core Knowledge; AP World/Euro; Holocaust curricula*  
> THE central fact, documented unflinchingly (moral_vision §4 'show the evil'; HISTORY_FRAMEWORK line 146). Industrialized murder of approximately six million Jews, plus Roma, Poles, Soviet POWs, the disabled, and others. State the scale and the method plainly — never sanitize, never theatricalize as a reveal. This is the bank's supreme example of where flinching from a hard topic is itself a failure.

**33. Anne Frank and the Diary** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T1-T4 · wt=high ⭐  
Hidden Jewish girl; diary in the Amsterdam annex · *src: Core Knowledge; cultural-literacy canon*  
> The human entry point (moral_vision §5 'children deserve moral formation'). One girl's voice makes six million comprehensible to a child. Story-led: the secret annex, the helpers who risked everything (Miep Gies hiding the diary), the betrayal, Bergen-Belsen. 'In spite of everything I still believe people are really good at heart' — handle with care, paired with the truth of what happened to her. The most accessible Holocaust topic for T1.

**34. Liberation of the Camps** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T3-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
1945 Allied troops discover and free the camps · *src: AP US/Euro; Holocaust curricula*  
> The moment the evidence became undeniable (moral_vision §4). Eisenhower ordering the atrocities filmed and German civilians marched through to see, specifically so no one could later deny it ('Get it all on record now... somewhere down the track of history some bastard will get up and say this never happened') — that act of deliberate documentation is the named, load-bearing detail. State what the soldiers found plainly.

**35. Righteous Rescuers (Schindler, Wallenberg)** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
Schindler's list; Wallenberg's passports save Jews · *src: Holocaust curricula; cultural-literacy canon*  
> VISION-CENTRAL: virtue through example at cost (moral_vision §2, §4 'courage in pursuit of truth'). Individuals who chose to save lives when it was lethal to do so — Schindler bankrupting himself, Raoul Wallenberg issuing thousands of protective passports in Budapest then vanishing into Soviet captivity (note the second totalitarian swallowing the hero). The bank shows the deed and lets it speak. HIGH weight — this is exactly the moral formation the vision exists for.

**36. Enigma and Bletchley Park Codebreaking** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
Turing breaks German cipher; Ultra intelligence · *src: AP Euro; cultural-literacy canon; Britannica*  
> Beauty of mechanism + drama of the great minds (moral_vision §2; HISTORY_FRAMEWORK pillar 6). Secret mathematics that shortened the war by an estimated two years and saved countless lives — Turing's bombe, the Poles (Rejewski) who cracked Enigma FIRST and gave their work to the Allies (an honest credit the popular telling drops). Turing's tragic postwar fate is part of the record, stated plainly, not theatricalized. The wonder is that abstract logic won battles.

**37. Navajo Code Talkers** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T3-T4 · wt=medium  
Marines use Navajo language as unbreakable code · *src: Core Knowledge Gr8; AP US*  
> Ingenuity + honest American complexity (moral_vision §2, §3.7). Native Americans whose own language became an unbreakable battlefield cipher — celebrate the achievement and the men. The bank may note without editorializing that some had been punished as children for speaking that same language in government schools; honest history holds both, no theatrical irony.

**38. Manhattan Project** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
Secret US atomic bomb program; Oppenheimer; Trinity · *src: Core Knowledge Gr8; AP US/World; Britannica*  
> American scientific achievement + moral weight (moral_vision §2, §3.6; HISTORY_FRAMEWORK pillar 6). The race to unlock the atom — Einstein's letter, Oppenheimer, the Trinity test, 'Now I am become Death, the destroyer of worlds.' Present the genuine scientific feat AND the gravity. Vision-relevant thread: Soviet espionage (Klaus Fuchs, the Rosenbergs) penetrated the project — honest history that ties into the coming Cold War. Drama of minds at the breaking point.

**39. Hiroshima and Nagasaki** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T2-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
Aug 1945 atomic bombings end the war · *src: Core Knowledge Gr8; AP US/World; Britannica*  
> Hard topic, honest not preachy (moral_vision §4). State the facts: the only wartime use of atomic weapons, the immediate and lingering deaths, and the genuine decision-context (Okinawa's casualty preview, the projected cost of invading Japan). Present the real moral argument on its own terms WITHOUT imposing a verdict — the bank states what happened and the competing considerations, and lets the player weigh them. No 'we now know better' framing; no glibness about the suffering (viral test).

**40. The American Home Front (Rosie the Riveter)** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T1-T4 · wt=medium  
War production, rationing, women in factories · *src: Core Knowledge Gr8; AP US*  
> The arsenal of democracy (moral_vision §3.6; HISTORY_FRAMEWORK pillar 2). The astonishing free-society production miracle — a continent retooling, Liberty ships built in days, women filling the factories. 'We Can Do It!' as cultural canon. Celebrate the mobilization of a free people honestly; the productive capacity of the American system is itself part of why fascism lost.

**41. Japanese-American Internment** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
120,000 forced into camps; Korematsu case · *src: Core Knowledge Gr8; AP US*  
> American failure stated plainly, NOT theatricalized (moral_vision §3.6, §9 'not triumphalist'). This is explicitly on the vision's named list of American injustices to state as fact. 120,000 people, two-thirds citizens, imprisoned without charge; Korematsu (and the later coram nobis vacatur) as the civil-liberties failure. Pair honestly with the 442nd Regimental Combat Team — Japanese-American soldiers who became among the most decorated units in U.S. history while their families were behind wire. Both truths, no reveal-staging.

**42. Tuskegee Airmen** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T2-T4 · wt=medium ⭐  
First Black US military pilots; escort record · *src: Core Knowledge Gr8; AP US*  
> Virtue-through-example against injustice at home (moral_vision §2, §3.7; named in the celebrate list, line 53). The first Black American military pilots, whose excellence — the bomber-escort record — was achieved while fighting segregation in their own country. Celebrate the achievement and the men; the injustice they overcame is stated as fact, part of the same honest American record. Civil-rights tradition as moral victory.

**43. European Resistance Movements** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T4-T5 · wt=medium  
French Resistance, partisans, sabotage, spies · *src: AP Euro Unit 8; Britannica*  
> Ordinary courage under occupation (moral_vision §2, §4). Maquis, the SOE 'setting Europe ablaze', the Norwegian heavy-water sabotage at Vemork (a genuinely cinematic, true story that denied Hitler atomic material), the Danish rescue of nearly all its Jews by fishing boat. Virtue-through-deed. Honor the resisters specifically and by named operation rather than as a faceless category.

**44. Rape of Nanjing** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T4-T5 · wt=medium  
1937 Japanese atrocity in China; mass killing · *src: AP World Unit 7; Britannica*  
> Atrocity stated as fact, no flinching (moral_vision §4; HISTORY_FRAMEWORK pillar 5 honest-brutality discipline). The mass killing and rape in Nanjing 1937 is the Asian war's pre-1941 horror kids should not miss. Vision-aligned addition the consensus scope omits: John Rabe, the German businessman (and Nazi Party member) who led the Nanjing Safety Zone and sheltered ~200,000 Chinese — a complicated rescuer worth naming. State the atrocity plainly, no sensationalism, no anti-culture framing.

**45. Strategic Bombing and Tokyo Firebombing** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T4-T5 · wt=medium  
Dresden and Tokyo raids; mass civilian deaths · *src: AP World/Euro; Britannica*  
> Hard moral question presented WITHOUT a verdict (moral_vision §4, viral test). State the facts — the firebombing of Dresden, the March 1945 Tokyo raid that killed roughly 100,000 in one night. Present the genuine debate (military necessity vs the morality of area bombing of cities) on its own terms; do not impose 'we now know better,' and do not be glib about the dead. This is exactly the kind of contested-ethics topic the bank handles by stating, not preaching.

**46. Mussolini and Fascist Italy** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=medium ⭐  
Il Duce; fascism's origin; 1945 execution · *src: AP Euro Unit 8; Britannica*  
> Anti-totalitarianism in every form (moral_vision §1.2). The ORIGINAL fascist regime — define fascism as the actual historical ideology (corporatist, ultranationalist, anti-liberal) so the kid knows what the word genuinely means, not as a generic insult. Mussolini's march on Rome, the cult of Il Duce, the grim end at Lake Como 1945. Present the doctrine seriously enough to be recognized and rejected on the merits.

**47. The Big Three (Yalta and Potsdam)** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T4-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
Roosevelt, Churchill, Stalin plan the postwar world · *src: AP World/Euro/US; Britannica*  
> VISION-LOAD-BEARING bridge to the Cold War (moral_vision §3.6; HISTORY_FRAMEWORK line 146 'honest about post-war carve-up', pillar 4). Yalta where the fate of Eastern Europe was effectively conceded to Stalin, and the betrayal of Poland — the nation whose invasion started the war handed to a new tyranny. State honestly that the defeat of one totalitarianism left half a continent under another. This seeds the Cold War as moral conflict; do not present Yalta as a clean diplomatic triumph.

**48. FDR's Wartime Leadership** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T2-T5 · wt=medium  
Four-term president; Lend-Lease; 'arsenal of democracy' · *src: Core Knowledge Gr8; AP US*  
> American story, flawed-but-extraordinary leadership (moral_vision §3.6; HISTORY_FRAMEWORK pillar 2, line 28 'flawed humans who built something extraordinary'). The president who marshaled the arsenal of democracy and Lend-Lease. Celebrate the wartime leadership honestly while not sanitizing — internment was signed by his hand (Executive Order 9066), and his trust of Stalin at Yalta is fairly questioned. Show the man, not the monument.

**49. Allied Commanders (Eisenhower, Patton, MacArthur)** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T2-T5 · wt=medium  
Key US generals of Europe and the Pacific · *src: Core Knowledge Gr8; AP US; Britannica*  
> Story-led generalship, real characters (HISTORY_FRAMEWORK pillar 3, pillar 6 drama). Eisenhower the coalition-holding statesman-general (and his D-Day integrity note); Patton the profane armored genius; MacArthur's 'I shall return.' Present them as the vivid, flawed, formidable personalities they were — the human drama is the hook, not a hagiography. Named moments and quotes are the memorable detail.

**50. V-E Day and V-J Day** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T2-T4 · wt=medium  
May & Aug-Sept 1945 victory celebrations; surrenders · *src: Core Knowledge Gr8; AP US; Britannica*  
> The victory of free peoples over tyranny, celebrated honestly (moral_vision §2). The formal surrenders (the deck of the USS Missouri for V-J Day), the jubilation. A legitimate moment of celebration in the bank — the defeat of fascism was a genuine good. Pair lightly with the sober truth that for Eastern Europe, one occupation simply replaced another (links to the Cold War strand without over-loading the celebration).

**51. Nuremberg Trials** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T4-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
1945-46 Nazi leaders tried for crimes against humanity · *src: AP World/Euro; Britannica*  
> The Western legal tradition applied to atrocity (moral_vision §3.4 rule of law, §3.6). The birth of the principle that 'I was following orders' is no defense and that leaders answer for crimes against humanity — a real achievement of the Anglo-American legal inheritance. Justice Jackson's opening is the named anchor. Honest-history footnote: the Soviet judges sat in judgment over crimes (Katyn) their own regime had committed — state plainly, no theatrical irony.

**52. Casualties and Scale of WWII** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T3-T4 · wt=medium  
~70-85M dead; deadliest conflict in history · *src: AP World Unit 7; Britannica*  
> The human cost stated plainly, singular memorable framing (moral_vision §4; SHARED_PRINCIPLES single-number discipline). The deadliest conflict in human history. Vision overlay: be honest that the majority of the dead were civilians and that the USSR and China bore the largest losses — and that a large share of those Soviet deaths trace to Stalin's own conduct (purged officer corps, Order 227, deportations), not the Germans alone. The scale is the wonder; the honest accounting of who died and why is the discipline.

**53. The Katyn Forest Massacre** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T4-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
1940 Soviet NKVD murder of ~22,000 Polish officers, police, and intelligentsia; the decades-long Soviet lie blaming the Nazis · *src: vision-injected*  
> VISION-REQUIRED, consensus-omitted. The framework explicitly names Katyn (line 106). This is the keystone WWII Soviet atrocity: the NKVD executed roughly 22,000 captured Polish officers and elites in 1940, then blamed Germany — a lie the USSR maintained until 1990 and the West partly tolerated for alliance convenience. Anti-totalitarianism in EVERY form (§1.2) demands this sit beside the Nazi atrocities; an Anglo-American consensus list that records only Axis crimes during WWII fails the vision. State the murder, the cover-up, and the Western complicity in the silence plainly.

**54. Soviet Mass Deportations and the Brutal Occupation of the East** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T4-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
NKVD deportation of Poles, Balts, and others to the Gulag 1939-41; the 1944-45 Red Army occupation — mass rape, looting, and fresh terror — as the front moved west · *src: vision-injected*  
> VISION-REQUIRED, consensus under-covered. The war in the East had TWO totalitarian occupiers. From 1939 the Soviets deported hundreds of thousands of Poles, Estonians, Latvians, and Lithuanians to Siberia and the Gulag; in 1944-45 the Red Army's advance brought mass rape (estimates in the hundreds of thousands to over a million women) and a new reign of terror. The consensus list shows the Nazi occupation's horrors but is silent on the Soviet half. State both occupations plainly, no false-equivalence-erasure — for Poland and the Baltics, liberation from Hitler was the start of a second tyranny.

**55. Christian and Student Resistance of Conscience (Bonhoeffer, the White Rose)** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=medium ⭐  
Dietrich Bonhoeffer and the Confessing Church; Hans and Sophie Scholl and the White Rose student leaflets; faith- and conscience-driven defiance of the regime from inside Germany · *src: vision-injected*  
> VISION-REQUIRED, consensus-omitted. The vision celebrates courage-at-cost and treats Christianity as a substantive moral-historical agent (HISTORY_FRAMEWORK line 147; moral_vision §2, §3.4). Bonhoeffer — the pastor who opposed Hitler from conscience, joined the resistance, and was hanged at Flossenbürg days before liberation; Sophie and Hans Scholl of the White Rose, guillotined at 21 and 24 for distributing anti-Nazi leaflets in Munich — are the bank's archetypal virtue-through-deed figures. The consensus list has external rescuers (Schindler/Wallenberg) but no internal German resistance of conscience and no faith dimension. Show the deed; let it speak; never preach it.

**56. The Forgotten Allies of the East (Polish Pilots, Polish Codebreakers, the 1944 Warsaw Rising)** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T4-T5 · wt=medium ⭐  
Polish 303 Squadron in the Battle of Britain; the Polish mathematicians who broke Enigma first; the 1944 Warsaw Rising and the Red Army's deliberate halt across the Vistula · *src: vision-injected*  
> VISION-REQUIRED, consensus under-covered. The Allies of the East are the war's most-betrayed heroes and the bank should name them. Polish 303 Squadron was the highest-scoring RAF fighter unit in the Battle of Britain; Polish mathematicians (Rejewski, Różycki, Zygalski) broke Enigma BEFORE Bletchley and handed the method to the Allies. The 1944 Warsaw Rising: the Home Army rose against the Germans while the Red Army sat across the river and deliberately let the Nazis annihilate the Polish resistance Stalin would otherwise have had to subjugate himself. Honor the courage; state the Soviet betrayal plainly. Anti-totalitarianism + virtue-through-deed in a single strand.

**57. The Iron Curtain Descends (Origins of the Cold War from the Ashes)** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T4-T5 · wt=medium ⭐  
The post-1945 Soviet imposition of communist regimes across Eastern Europe; Churchill's 1946 'Iron Curtain' speech at Fulton naming the new division of Europe · *src: vision-injected*  
> VISION-REQUIRED bridge; the consensus list stops too early. It ends WWII at V-J Day / Nuremberg as a clean Allied triumph. The vision (HISTORY_FRAMEWORK pillar 4, line 146) insists the war's true ending includes the descent of half of Europe into a SECOND tyranny — Soviet-installed regimes from Poland to Bulgaria — and Churchill at Fulton 1946: 'From Stettin in the Baltic to Trieste in the Adriatic, an iron curtain has descended across the Continent.' Frame this as the seed of the Cold War as moral conflict. The defeat of fascism was a real good; it was not the end of totalitarianism in Europe. Keeps the WWII strand honest rather than triumphalist.


## The Cold War & the modern world (47)

**1. Iron Curtain & Churchill's Fulton Speech** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T2-T3 · wt=high ⭐  
Churchill names the Soviet divide of Europe · *src: Core Knowledge CKHG; AP Euro postwar*  
> Story-led: Churchill out of power, March 1946 Fulton Missouri, the phrase 'an iron curtain has descended across the Continent.' Present the divide as what it was — free West vs. Soviet-imposed captivity, not a neutral 'two blocs.' Stem carries the quote; answer is the named image. Churchill saw the totalitarian threat clearly when others wanted to trust Stalin.

**2. Truman Doctrine & Containment** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T2-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
US policy to contain communist expansion · *src: AP US Since 1945; AP World 8.2*  
> Present as the strategic spine of 40 years of the moral conflict. Truman 1947, Greece and Turkey, Kennan's 'Long Telegram' and the 'X' article as the intellectual origin (T4-T5). Frame containment as resisting a regime the vision treats as genuinely oppressive — not 'American aggression.' Steelman both the policy and its critics (overreach) without false equivalence.

**3. Marshall Plan** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T2-T4 · wt=high ⭐  
US aid rebuilds postwar Western Europe · *src: Core Knowledge; AP Euro; AP World*  
> Celebrate as American generosity and strategic wisdom — $13B rebuilt the free West and inoculated it against communism. Pairs with the §3.2 economic lens: market recovery vs. the command-economy stagnation behind the Curtain. The contrast (West rebuilt and prospered; the East stagnated) is honest history, the seen-vs-unseen made visible across a continent.

**4. Berlin Blockade & Airlift** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T2-T4 · wt=high ⭐  
West Berlin supplied entirely by air 1948-49 · *src: AP Euro; AP US Since 1945*  
> Wonder + virtue: a plane landing every ~45 seconds, 'candy bombers' (Gail Halvorsen, Operation Little Vittles) dropping sweets to children. Frame as the free world feeding a city Stalin tried to starve into submission — courage and ingenuity against coercion. The cool fact (the cadence, the candy parachutes) is the answer.

**5. NATO vs. Warsaw Pact** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T2-T4 · wt=medium  
Rival military alliances split the world · *src: AP World 8.2; Core Knowledge*  
> Distinguish the two camps honestly: NATO a voluntary alliance of free nations; the Warsaw Pact the instrument by which Moscow held captive nations in line (it was Warsaw Pact tanks that crushed Hungary 1956 and Prague 1968). Not a symmetric 'both had alliances' framing — name what each actually did.

**6. Chinese Revolution & Mao 1949** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
Communists win China; Mao takes power · *src: AP World 8.4; AP US*  
> This is a gateway to the §3.1 atrocity record — do NOT let it end at '1949 Mao wins.' The stem sets the takeover; the heaviest content (Great Leap Forward 30-45M, Cultural Revolution) lives in its own injected ladder. Chang & Halliday's Mao is the cited scholarship. Honest count, named perpetrator, no romanticizing the revolution.

**7. Korean War & the 38th Parallel** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T2-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
Hot war splits Korea; UN forces fight · *src: AP US Since 1945; AP World 8.2*  
> The first shooting war of the moral conflict. Inchon landing (MacArthur's gamble), the Chinese intervention, the stalemate at the 38th. Frame the outcome morally legible: South Korea became free and prosperous; the North a totalitarian prison state (sets up the injected NK-camps topic). The divided peninsula at night — lights vs. darkness — is the wonder hook.

**8. McCarthyism & the Red Scare** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
Hunt for communists inside America · *src: AP US Since 1945*  
> DUAL COMMITMENT held sharply (this is the hard path the vision demands). McCarthyism is named in moral_vision §3.6 and §9 as a genuine American FAILURE — blacklists, ruined innocents, demagoguery — stated plainly, Army-McCarthy hearings, 'Have you no sense of decency?' AND it is also true that Soviet espionage was real (Venona decrypts, Hiss, the Rosenbergs passed atomic secrets). Teach the kid to hold both: the spies were real AND the witch-hunt was an injustice. No false equivalence in either direction.

**9. The Space Race & Sputnik** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T2-T5 · wt=medium  
US-Soviet competition to reach space · *src: Core Knowledge; AP US*  
> Wonder-first. Sputnik's October 1957 beep heard on shortwave radios worldwide — the shock that a totalitarian rival had reached orbit first. Gagarin, then the American response. Frame as the free world mobilizing its open science to catch and surpass a closed one. The specific cool fact (the beeping signal, 'the night the sky changed') is the answer.

**10. Apollo 11 Moon Landing 1969** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T1-T4 · wt=high ⭐  
Americans first walk on the Moon · *src: Core Knowledge; AP US*  
> CELEBRATE — moral_vision §2 and §3.6 name landing humans on the Moon as a signature American achievement of the open society. 'One small step,' the 1202 alarm, Armstrong piloting past the boulder field with seconds of fuel. The free world won the race to the Moon. Wonder + American-experiment pillar both fire here. High billing.

**11. Cuban Revolution & Fidel Castro** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=medium ⭐  
Castro brings communism to Cuba 1959 · *src: AP World 8.4; AP US*  
> Do not romanticize the revolution. Frame honestly: Castro's takeover brought a one-party state 90 miles from Florida, and the La Cabaña executions (injected separately) followed. Che Guevara presented as the executioner he was, not the t-shirt icon. The mass exile of Cubans (the rafts, Miami) is part of the record. Honest, not cynical — state what the regime did.

**12. Cuban Missile Crisis 1962** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T2-T5 · wt=high  
Thirteen days at brink of nuclear war · *src: AP US Since 1945; AP World*  
> High drama, present straight — thirteen days, the U-2 photos, the quarantine line, Vasili Arkhipov's submarine veto (T5 wonder: one Soviet officer's 'no' may have stopped a nuclear launch). The closest the world came to nuclear war is a fact, not a thesis. Let the suspense carry it; the named decisive detail is the answer.

**13. Nuclear Arms Race & MAD** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=medium  
Hydrogen bombs, missiles, mutual destruction doctrine · *src: AP World 8.3; AP Euro*  
> Explain the grim logic of Mutual Assured Destruction as the mechanism it was — why two enemies each built world-ending arsenals (the beauty/horror of mechanism, §2). Connects to Sakharov (injected): the man who built the Soviet H-bomb then turned dissident against it. Tsar Bomba (50 megatons) as the staggering specific. No editorial hand-wringing — the facts carry the weight.

**14. The Berlin Wall Goes Up 1961** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T2-T4 · wt=high ⭐  
East Germany walls off West Berlin · *src: AP Euro; Core Knowledge*  
> Frame the wall correctly per §framework-line-16: it was built to keep people IN — a regime that must imprison its own citizens has confessed its nature. The barbed wire overnight in August 1961, families split mid-street, the first escapes and the first deaths (Peter Fechter bleeding at the wall). The Berlin Wall as an evil that needed breaking — stated as the bank's settled view of a totalitarian act, not 'a Cold War tension.'

**15. Berlin Wall & 'Tear Down This Wall'** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T3-T4 · wt=high ⭐  
Reagan's Brandenburg Gate challenge to Gorbachev · *src: AP US Since 1945*  
> CELEBRATE explicitly — HISTORY_FRAMEWORK §4 names Reagan at Brandenburg June 12 1987 as a Cold War moral high point. His own staff cut the line repeatedly; he kept it. 'Mr. Gorbachev, tear down this wall!' The quote IS the answer. Part of the Reagan-Thatcher-JP2 coalition the vision says to celebrate. The moral clarity is the point.

**16. Vietnam War** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=high  
US fights communism in Southeast Asia · *src: AP US Since 1945; AP World*  
> Present honestly and without either myth: a costly, divisive war fought to contain communism. State the real costs (58,000 American dead, the draft, the home-front division) AND the real stakes — what followed the US withdrawal was not peace but the Khmer Rouge killing fields next door and the boat-people exodus from a unified communist Vietnam. Honest, not cynical; no 'imperial aggression' framing, no jingoist whitewash. The consequence of withdrawal belongs in the record.

**17. Hungarian Revolution 1956** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T3-T4 · wt=high ⭐  
Soviet tanks crush a Hungarian uprising · *src: AP Euro; AP World 8.7*  
> A captive nation rising for freedom, crushed by Soviet tanks — exactly the moral conflict the vision foregrounds. Students at the radio station, the toppled Stalin statue (only the boots left), Imre Nagy executed, the West watching and not intervening. Frame the courage and the betrayal plainly. Sets the pattern the injected 'captive nations' thread completes.

**18. Prague Spring 1968** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T3-T4 · wt=high ⭐  
Czech reform crushed by Warsaw Pact tanks · *src: AP Euro; AP World 8.7*  
> 'Socialism with a human face' (Dubcek) rolled back by Warsaw Pact tanks — Jan Palach's self-immolation in protest is the searing specific. Connect forward to Havel (injected): the playwright-dissident this crackdown forged. Frame as another captive-nation rising betrayed — the system could not tolerate even reform from within.

**19. Decolonization of India 1947** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=medium  
Independence and Partition of British India · *src: AP World 8.5*  
> Honest both ways: the largest colony wins self-rule (a genuine good) AND Partition unleashed 1-2M deaths and the largest migration in history. State the violence plainly (§4 honest-not-cynical). Non-Western actors on their own terms — Nehru, Jinnah, the Radcliffe Line drawn in weeks. Not a morality play about the West; the human cost of the partition is the sober center.

**20. Gandhi & Nonviolent Independence** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T2-T5 · wt=medium ⭐  
Salt March, civil disobedience against British rule · *src: Core Knowledge; AP World*  
> Virtue-by-example (§2, §5): the man who beat an empire without weapons. The Salt March to the sea (240 miles, picking up a handful of salt as defiance), satyagraha defined inline. Honest at T5: Gandhi's method worked AGAINST a Britain with a conscience and a free press — a candor the vision's intellectual seriousness invites without diminishing the achievement. The named act (the salt) is the answer.

**21. African Decolonization & Ghana 1957** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=medium  
African nations win independence from Europe · *src: AP World 8.5*  
> Non-Western self-rule on its own terms (§framework pillar 6) — Nkrumah, Ghana first in 1957, the 'wind of change.' Present honestly, neither romanticized nor cynical: real liberation, and the hard road of building states afterward. Avoid both colonial-apologia and the inverse anti-Western condemnation framing (§9). The independence moment is the wonder; the named first nation is the answer.

**22. Suez Crisis 1956** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T4-T5 · wt=low  
Egypt nationalizes canal; Britain's power wanes · *src: AP World; AP Euro*  
> The moment the old empires lost the world stage — Britain and France humiliated when the US refused to back them, the torch passing to the two superpowers. Present as the structural shift it was (T4-T5 analyst register). Nasser, the canal, the Eisenhower pressure. No editorializing; the geopolitical reveal is the substance.

**23. Apartheid & Nelson Mandela** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T2-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
South African racial system and its fall · *src: AP World 8.5; Core Knowledge*  
> Racism against any group is morally unacceptable (§3.7) — apartheid stated plainly as the injustice it was. Mandela: 27 years on Robben Island, then president; the Rugby World Cup 1995 reconciliation gesture as virtue-by-example. Frame the fall as a moral victory AND honor Mandela's choice of reconciliation over revenge — the harder, nobler path. The specific (27 years; the number on his prison block) is the answer.

**24. Korean & Berlin Spy Drama / Espionage** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=medium  
Spies, U-2 incident, Rosenbergs, defectors · *src: AP US Since 1945*  
> The secret war — wonder-rich. Powers' U-2 shot down 1960 (and the poison pin he didn't use), the Glienicke 'Bridge of Spies' exchanges, Kim Philby and the Cambridge Five. On the Rosenbergs, follow §3.6/McCarthy dual-commitment honesty: Venona decrypts later confirmed Julius's guilt — the espionage was real. Named figure or named operation is the answer; no fabricated tradecraft.

**25. Gorbachev: Glasnost & Perestroika** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
Soviet reforms of openness and restructuring · *src: AP Euro; AP US Since 1945*  
> The reforms that accidentally unraveled the USSR — present the irony straight: a system that could not survive even partial honesty (glasnost) about itself. Define both terms inline. Frame within the moral conflict: the Western pressure (Reagan's arms buildup, the economic exhaustion per §3.2) is WHY reform became necessary — Gorbachev did not act in a vacuum. Honest about him as a reformer-Communist, not a liberator by intent.

**26. Solidarity & the Polish Workers' Movement** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T4-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
Walesa's union cracks communism in Poland · *src: AP Euro; AP World 8.7*  
> CELEBRATE — HISTORY_FRAMEWORK §4 names Solidarity, Walesa, and JP2 as heroes of the Cold War. The Gdansk shipyard strike, Walesa the electrician climbing the fence, 10 million members, martial law 1981, then victory in the 1989 elections. CRITICAL: pair with JP2's June 1979 Warsaw homily ('Be not afraid') as the spark — that linkage is explicitly vision-mandated. Workers and a pope toppled a regime. The named spark/figure is the answer.

**27. Fall of the Berlin Wall 1989** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T1-T4 · wt=high ⭐  
Crowds open the wall; East-West reunite · *src: AP Euro; Core Knowledge*  
> The night the evil that needed breaking finally fell (§framework-line-16). November 9 1989 — Schabowski's bungled press conference ('immediately, without delay'), crowds swarming Checkpoint Charlie, the hammers and the wall-peckers. CELEBRATE without restraint: this is the moral conflict's triumphant payoff. The specific (the accidental announcement that opened the gates) is the answer. High billing, accessible from T1.

**28. Collapse of the Soviet Union 1991** — **DEEP** (~12q) · `ladder` · T3-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
USSR dissolves into independent states · *src: AP World 8.8; AP US*  
> A superpower vanishes — December 25 1991, the red flag lowered over the Kremlin for the last time, the failed August coup, Yeltsin on the tank. Frame as the moral conflict's verdict: the open society outlasted the closed one, the West bankrupted the USSR (economic-exhaustion thread, §3.2). Not 'a tragedy,' not gloating — the empire that built the Gulag ended. The named final image is the answer.

**29. Tiananmen Square 1989** — standalone (~2q) · `standalone` · T3-T5 · wt=high ⭐  
China crushes pro-democracy protest; Tank Man · *src: AP World 8.7*  
> Explicitly in the §3.1 atrocity record AND HISTORY_FRAMEWORK §3 ('Tiananmen Square June 4 1989 + Tank Man'). One man, the grocery bags, the column of tanks — the frozen image is the answer. State the crackdown plainly: the regime massacred its own citizens demanding freedom. Honest, unflinching; the courage and the brutality both stated as fact. Vision-mandated, high.

**30. Soviet War in Afghanistan** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T4-T5 · wt=medium  
USSR's costly decade-long invasion and defeat · *src: AP World; AP US Since 1945*  
> The 'Soviet Vietnam' that drained the empire — present as a contributing cause of the collapse (ties to the economic-exhaustion thread). The mujahideen, the Stinger missiles, 15,000 Soviet dead, the 1989 withdrawal. Honest at T5 about the long downstream consequences without dragging in post-2010 current politics (§6 dated-references line). The defeat that helped break the USSR is the substance.

**31. Founding of the United Nations 1945** — standard (~3q) · `ladder` · T2-T4 · wt=low  
Postwar body to keep world peace · *src: Core Knowledge; AP World*  
> Present on its own terms — the world's attempt to prevent another world war, San Francisco 1945, the Security Council and the veto. Honest, not idealized: the veto meant the USSR could block action, and the UN watched Hungary 1956 and Prague 1968 without stopping the tanks. State what it was designed to do and where the structure limited it. No advocacy either for or against.

**32. Creation of Israel 1948** — standard (~5q) · `ladder` · T4-T5 · wt=medium  
Jewish state founded; Arab-Israeli conflict begins · *src: AP World 8.6*  
> Present straight as historical fact (T4-T5): the founding May 1948 in the shadow of the Holocaust, Ben-Gurion's declaration, the immediate war.
